309:1:# Graphiti MCP Server
310-2:
311:3:Graphiti is a framework for building and querying temporally-aware knowledge graphs, specifically tailored for AI agents
312:4:operating in dynamic environments. Unlike traditional retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods, Graphiti
313-5:continuously integrates user interactions, structured and unstructured enterprise data, and external information into a
314-6:coherent, queryable graph. The framework supports incremental data updates, efficient retrieval, and precise historical
315-7:queries without requiring complete graph recomputation, making it suitable for developing interactive, context-aware AI
316-8:applications.
317-9:
318:10:This is an experimental Model Context Protocol (MCP) server implementation for Graphiti. The MCP server exposes
319:11:Graphiti's key functionality through the MCP protocol, allowing AI assistants to interact with Graphiti's knowledge
320-12:graph capabilities.
321-13:
322-14:## Features
323-15:
324:16:The Graphiti MCP server provides comprehensive knowledge graph capabilities:
325-17:
326-18:- **Episode Management**: Add, retrieve, and delete episodes (text, messages, or JSON data)
327-19:- **Entity Management**: Search and manage entity nodes and relationships in the knowledge graph
328-20:- **Search Capabilities**: Search for facts (edges) and node summaries using semantic and hybrid search
--
336-28:- **Queue-based Processing**: Asynchronous episode processing with configurable concurrency limits
337-29:
338-30:## Quick Start
339-31:
340:32:### Clone the Graphiti GitHub repo
341-33:

343:35:git clone https://github.com/getzep/graphiti.git
344-36:```
345-37:
346-38:or
347-39:
348-40:```bash
349:41:gh repo clone getzep/graphiti
350-42:```

358-50:```

361-53:

363-55:

500-192:### Entity Types
501-193:
502:194:Graphiti MCP Server includes built-in entity types for structured knowledge extraction. These entity types are always enabled and configured via the `entity_types` section in your `config.yaml`:
503-195:
504-196:**Available Entity Types:**
505-197:
506-198:- **Preference**: User preferences, choices, opinions, or selections (prioritized for user-specific information)
--
515-207:
516-208:These entity types are defined in `config.yaml` and can be customized by modifying the descriptions:
517-209:
518-210:```yaml
519:211:graphiti:
520-212:  entity_types:
521-213:    - name: "Preference"
522-214:      description: "User preferences, choices, opinions, or selections"
523-215:    - name: "Requirement"

552-244:## Running the Server
553-245:
554-246:### Default Setup (FalkorDB Combined Container)
555-247:
556:248:To run the Graphiti MCP server with the default FalkorDB setup:
557-249:
558-250:```bash
559-251:docker compose up
560-252:```
561-253:
562-254:This starts a single container with:
563-255:- HTTP transport on `http://localhost:8000/mcp/`
564:256:- FalkorDB graph database on `localhost:6379`
565-257:- FalkorDB web UI on `http://localhost:3000`
566-258:- OpenAI LLM with gpt-5-mini model

616-308:uv run main.py --database-provider falkordb
617-309:```
--
631-323:- `--model`: Model name to use with the LLM client
632-324:- `--temperature`: Temperature setting for the LLM (0.0-2.0)
633-325:- `--transport`: Choose the transport method (http or stdio, default: http)
634-326:- `--group-id`: Set a namespace for the graph (optional). If not provided, defaults to "main"
635:327:- `--destroy-graph`: If set, destroys all Graphiti graphs on startup
636-328:
637-329:### Concurrency and LLM Provider 429 Rate Limit Errors
638-330:
639:331:Graphiti's ingestion pipelines are designed for high concurrency, controlled by the `SEMAPHORE_LIMIT` environment variable. This setting determines how many episodes can be processed simultaneously. Since each episode involves multiple LLM calls (entity extraction, deduplication, summarization), the actual number of concurrent LLM requests will be several times higher.
640-332:
641-333:**Default:** `SEMAPHORE_LIMIT=10` (suitable for OpenAI Tier 3, mid-tier Anthropic)
642-334:
643-335:#### Tuning Guidelines by LLM Provider
--
678-370:```
679-371:

683-375:

795-487:
796-488:### Other MCP Clients
797-489:
798:490:To use the Graphiti MCP server with other MCP-compatible clients, configure it to connect to the server:
799-491:
800-492:> [!IMPORTANT]
801-493:> You will need the Python package manager, `uv` installed. Please refer to the [`uv` install instructions](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/getting-started/installation/).
802-494:>
803:495:> Ensure that you set the full path to the `uv` binary and your Graphiti project folder.
804-496:
805-497:```json
806-498:{
807-499:  "mcpServers": {
808:500:    "graphiti-memory": {
809-501:      "transport": "stdio",
810-502:      "command": "/Users/<user>/.local/bin/uv",
811-503:      "args": [
812-504:        "run",
813-505:        "--isolated",
814-506:        "--directory",
815:507:        "/Users/<user>>/dev/zep/graphiti/mcp_server",
816-508:        "--project",
817-509:        ".",
818-510:        "main.py",
819-511:        "--transport",
--
835-527:
836-528:```json
837-529:{
838-530:  "mcpServers": {
839:531:    "graphiti-memory": {
840-532:      "transport": "http",
841-533:      "url": "http://localhost:8000/mcp/"
842-534:    }
843-535:  }
--
845-537:```
846-538:
847-539:## Available Tools
848-540:
849:541:The Graphiti MCP server exposes the following tools:
850-542:
851-543:- `add_episode`: Add an episode to the knowledge graph (supports text, JSON, and message formats)
852-544:- `search_nodes`: Search the knowledge graph for relevant node summaries
853-545:- `search_facts`: Search the knowledge graph for relevant facts (edges between entities)
--
855-547:- `delete_episode`: Delete an episode from the knowledge graph
856-548:- `get_entity_edge`: Get an entity edge by its UUID
857-549:- `get_episodes`: Get the most recent episodes for a specific group
858-550:- `clear_graph`: Clear all data from the knowledge graph and rebuild indices
859:551:- `get_status`: Get the status of the Graphiti MCP server and Neo4j connection
860-552:
861-553:## Working with JSON Data
862-554:
863:555:The Graphiti MCP server can process structured JSON data through the `add_episode` tool with `source="json"`. This
864-556:allows you to automatically extract entities and relationships from structured data:
865-557:
866-558:```
867-559:
--
875-567:```
876-568:
877-569:## Integrating with the Cursor IDE
878-570:
879:571:To integrate the Graphiti MCP Server with the Cursor IDE, follow these steps:
880-572:
881:573:1. Run the Graphiti MCP server using the default HTTP transport:
882-574:
883-575:```bash
884-576:uv run main.py --group-id <your_group_id>
885-577:```
--
891-583:```bash
892-584:docker compose up
893-585:```
894-586:
895:587:2. Configure Cursor to connect to the Graphiti MCP server.
896-588:
897-589:```json
898-590:{
899-591:  "mcpServers": {
900:592:    "graphiti-memory": {
901-593:      "url": "http://localhost:8000/mcp/"
902-594:    }
903-595:  }
904-596:}
905-597:```
906-598:
907:599:3. Add the Graphiti rules to Cursor's User Rules. See [cursor_rules.md](cursor_rules.md) for details.
908-600:
909-601:4. Kick off an agent session in Cursor.
910-602:
911:603:The integration enables AI assistants in Cursor to maintain persistent memory through Graphiti's knowledge graph
912-604:capabilities.
913-605:
914-606:## Integrating with Claude Desktop (Docker MCP Server)
915-607:
916:608:The Graphiti MCP Server uses HTTP transport (at endpoint `/mcp/`). Claude Desktop does not natively support HTTP transport, so you'll need to use a gateway like `mcp-remote`.
917-609:
918:610:1.  **Run the Graphiti MCP server**:
919-611:
920-612:    ```bash
921-613:    docker compose up
922-614:    # Or run directly with uv:
--
935-627:
936-628:    ```json
937-629:    {
938-630:      "mcpServers": {
939:631:        "graphiti-memory": {
940-632:          // You can choose a different name if you prefer
941-633:          "command": "npx", // Or the full path to mcp-remote if npx is not in your PATH
942-634:          "args": [
943-635:            "mcp-remote",
944:636:            "http://localhost:8000/mcp/" // The Graphiti server's HTTP endpoint
945-637:          ]
946-638:        }
947-639:      }
948-640:    }
949-641:    ```
950-642:
951:643:    If you already have an `mcpServers` entry, add `graphiti-memory` (or your chosen name) as a new key within it.
952-644:
953-645:4.  **Restart Claude Desktop** for the changes to take effect.
954-646:
955-647:## Requirements
--
961-653:- (Optional) Neo4j database (version 5.26 or later) if not using the default FalkorDB setup
962-654:
963-655:## Telemetry
964-656:
965:657:The Graphiti MCP server uses the Graphiti core library, which includes anonymous telemetry collection. When you initialize the Graphiti MCP server, anonymous usage statistics are collected to help improve the framework.
966-658:
967-659:### What's Collected
968-660:
969-661:- Anonymous identifier and system information (OS, Python version)
970:662:- Graphiti version and configuration choices (LLM provider, database backend, embedder type)
971-663:- **No personal data, API keys, or actual graph content is ever collected**
972-664:
973-665:### How to Disable
974-666:
--
983-675:```
984-676:GRAPHITI_TELEMETRY_ENABLED=false
985-677:```
986-678:
987:679:For complete details about what's collected and why, see the [Telemetry section in the main Graphiti README](../README.md#telemetry).
988-680:

990-682:

import socket

def send_cmd(port, parts):
    payload = ('*%d\\r\\n' % len(parts)).encode()
    for part in parts:
        b = part.encode()
        payload += ('"'$%d'"\\r\\n' % len(b)).encode() + b + b'\\r\\n'
    try:
        with socket.create_connection(('127.0.0.1', port), timeout=2) as s:
            s.sendall(payload)
            s.settimeout(2)
            data = s.recv(4096)
            print(f'{port} {parts}: {data"'!r}'"')
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f'{port} {parts}: ERROR {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}')

for port in (6379, 16379):
    send_cmd(port, ['PING'])
    send_cmd(port, ['MODULE', 'LIST'])
    send_cmd(port, ['COMMAND', 'INFO', 'GRAPH.QUERY'])
'@ | python -" in Z:\claude-sota-installed

**Evaluating Redis configuration**

I’m considering that a wrong config edit in Redis can lead to an invalid database type. That suggests option A might be insufficient. I think it’s better to defer any edits for now. First, I’ll verify that the FalkorDB module is loaded on port 6379 by using the Python Redis client probe with MODULE LIST. Then, I’ll address the port fix only if it’s confirmed that the module is indeed loaded.
**Evaluating config options**

I'm looking at the options for verifying a configuration. Option B is the best because it checks things directly before making a decision. Option C opts for a choice without resolving some settings, and maybe it should consider restoring known good settings instead of editing. However, I'm wondering about the rule "FIRST restart Docker" — that might require someone to operate it, which contradicts "probe before edit." This complicates the understanding of whether an edit or environmental change is more suitable.
**Considering fallback options**

I'm thinking that if the module probe returns empty or gets no GRAPH.QUERY, it might be better to fall back on a C or operator decision instead of just going for a port fix. It feels like adding a "prescribed_edits": string array might be necessary if we identify things that need revision. I want to ensure I'm covering all bases here to keep everything running smoothly!

I need to ensure that the JSON string field for `rationale` contains 1 to 3 sentences. I can create a simple string with three clear sentences. I'll have to clarify that to ensure everything is properly structured. Let's proceed carefully with these considerations!
